## [FedLSG: LLM-Enhanced Federated Graph Backdoor Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19674) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedLSG is the first framework to integrate large language models (LLMs) into federated graph backdoor defense, using a graph and behavior to text grounding scheme and a student-teacher architecture. This addresses a critical security gap in federated graph learning, where existing rule-based defenses lack semantic understanding and are vulnerable to stealthy backdoor attacks. The server-side full-scale LLM acts as a teacher to evaluate client updates and identify malicious participants, while a LoRA-based student on the client side suppresses backdoor trigger edges.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Federated Graph Neural Networks (FedGNNs) enable collaborative learning on distributed graph data but are vulnerable to backdoor poisoning attacks. Existing defenses typically use rule-based methods that cannot understand semantic context, making them ineffective against stealthy triggers. FedLSG leverages LLMs' semantic reasoning capabilities to overcome this limitation.

## [Cross-Cultural Study on GenAI Ethics in Computing Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study reveals that Canadian university students are significantly more likely than South Korean students to view generative AI use in coding assignments as unethical and against institutional policies, despite identical policies at both institutions. This finding highlights the need for culturally responsive AI integration policies in global higher education, as uniform guidelines may not account for diverse ethical reasoning shaped by cultural dimensions like power distance and individualism. The study used a scenario-based survey in Fall 2024 and applied Mann-Whitney U tests to confirm significant cross-cultural differences. The amount of AI-generated code in assignments was the strongest factor influencing ethical judgments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI tools like ChatGPT have raised concerns about academic integrity in computing education. Hofstede's cultural dimensions framework explains how cultural values shape perceptions of rules and ethics. The Mann-Whitney U test is a nonparametric statistical method used to compare differences between two independent groups.

## [Arabic Dialect Geolocation via Continuous Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19751) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a regression-based neural network that predicts speaker origin as continuous latitude-longitude coordinates, achieving a median localization error of 481.2 km on Arabic dialect data. This work models Arabic dialect variation as a continuous space rather than discrete categories, providing a principled framework for dialect geolocation and quantitative support for the dialect continuum hypothesis. The model fuses XLS-R-300M and Whisper-large-v3 encoder representations with phonotactic descriptors via a Transformer encoder and learnable attention pooling, and uses a spherical geodesic loss to optimize great-circle distance directly.

## [RIS-Assisted Wireless FL with Adaptive Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19759) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a convergence-latency aware adaptive modulation and resource allocation scheme for RIS-assisted wireless federated learning, which jointly optimizes modulation order and sub-channel allocation to minimize training delay while ensuring convergence. This work bridges the gap between communication reliability and learning convergence in wireless FL, offering a practical framework that can significantly accelerate training in blocked or interference-prone environments, which is critical for deploying FL in real-world wireless systems. The authors derive a convergence upper bound that explicitly captures the impact of symbol error rate (SER) on FL loss decay, and formulate a mixed-integer nonlinear programming (MINLP) problem solved via a low-complexity hybrid alternating optimization. Experiments on MNIST, CIFAR-10, and Speech Commands show consistent improvements over baseline schemes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) trains a shared model across distributed clients without sharing raw data, but wireless transmission errors can degrade convergence and increase latency. Reconfigurable intelligent surfaces (RIS) are low-cost passive arrays that can reflect signals to improve link reliability, especially in blocked scenarios. Adaptive modulation adjusts the modulation order based on channel conditions to balance throughput and error rate, but its impact on FL convergence has not been well studied.

## [RPPNet: Perceptually-Grouped Primitives for Melody Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19776) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RPPNet introduces a two-stage deep learning architecture that generates variable-length Rhythm-Pitch Primitive (RPP) sequences based on perceptual grouping principles, then decodes them into concrete notes, achieving superior long-term structural coherence in symbolic melody generation. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of bar-level models by aligning generated structures with human musical perception, significantly improving musicality and long-term structure. It bridges music psychology, theory, and computational modeling, offering a new interdisciplinary direction for AI music generation. RPPNet uses acoustic cues, auditory inertia, and similarity perception to automatically group notes into RPPs, and ablation studies confirm that performance gains stem from the psychological representation's structural correctness rather than model capacity.

**Background**: Existing symbolic music generation models typically use fixed bar-length units, which often misalign with human perception of musical phrases, causing long-term structural fragmentation. Perceptual grouping, rooted in Gestalt psychology, describes how humans naturally organize auditory events into coherent patterns. RPPNet leverages these principles to define variable-length primitives that better reflect musical phrasing.

Researchers propose Auto-Fill, a method that post-trains three specialist small language models (SLMs) for world knowledge, text-based reasoning, and code-based reasoning, combined with a calibrated ensemble to predict missing table values with high accuracy and low cost. This approach achieves superior accuracy compared to frontier models like o3-pro and Gemini 3 Pro while costing less than 1% of their operational expense, making high-quality data cleaning accessible for large-scale tabular data. Auto-Fill uses a calibrated ensemble that either selects the most confident specialist or abstains from prediction, avoiding hallucinations. It was evaluated on 11 benchmarks with 2,200 real tables across diverse domains.

## [PRISM-DR: Per-Lesion Detection for Diabetic Retinopathy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19864) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PRISM-DR introduces a pipeline that trains separate YOLO detectors for each diabetic retinopathy lesion type, using per-lesion ensembling and inter-lesion suppression to improve detection of rare lesions. This approach addresses the class imbalance and diversity issues in diabetic retinopathy detection, potentially leading to earlier and more accurate diagnosis of the disease, which is a leading cause of preventable blindness. The system achieves a test mAP50 of 0.527 and F1 of 0.529 on the IDRiD dataset, with the highest AP50 of 0.561 on hard exudates. It uses five-fold cross-validation, Bayesian-optimized augmentation, and a novel inter-lesion suppression step that resolves overlaps based on lesion size and clinical priority.

Researchers propose DeCNIP, a defense that identifies and prunes backdoor critical neurons in LLMs, achieving over 95% reduction in attack success rate with only 0.1% neuron intervention. This addresses two critical gaps in LLM security: defending against model-editing backdoors that bypass training pipelines, and extending protection to open-ended generation tasks, which existing methods fail to handle. DeCNIP uses representational analysis to optimize cross-entropy loss between harmful prompts and benign inputs, isolating Backdoor Critical Neurons (BCNs) for selective pruning while preserving 97% of normal performance.

DeCNIP provides a mechanistic understanding by analyzing neuron-level representations.

## [OSVE: One-Step Video Editing with Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OSVE is the first framework to adapt one-step text-to-image diffusion models for video editing, achieving high-quality results with a learned encoder for inversion and Unified-Frame Editing for temporal consistency. OSVE accelerates video editing by 155–171 times compared to multi-step methods, making real-time video editing practical and significantly reducing computational cost. The learnable encoder predicts initial noise in a single forward pass, trained with a Structure-Aware Editing loss on structurally-aligned image pairs. Unified-Frame Editing concatenates frame latents for cross-frame attention, and a sliding-window strategy with an anchor frame maintains global consistency for long videos.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional video editing with diffusion models requires iterative multi-step sampling and inversion, which is slow and computationally expensive. One-step diffusion models condense the denoising process into a single pass, but adapting them to video editing poses challenges in inversion, editability, and temporal consistency. OSVE addresses these challenges with novel techniques.

## [NCIP: Neural Collapse Inspired Test Prioritization for DNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20046) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes NCIP, a neural-collapse-inspired prioritization framework that uses cross-checkpoint prediction variability instead of single-checkpoint confidence to prioritize test cases for deep neural networks. This approach addresses the limitation of confidence-based methods that can be confidently wrong, improving early fault discovery in safety-critical AI systems. It achieves 1.5–16.6% RAUC-ALL gains and 4.9–20.6% RAUC-500 gains over baselines. NCIP selects a representative subset of training checkpoints using an equiangularity score of classifier weights, then prioritizes test inputs by their prediction variability across those checkpoints. Experiments span multiple datasets and architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Test case prioritization for DNNs aims to order test inputs so that faults are discovered early under limited labeling budgets. Existing methods often rely on confidence scores from a single model checkpoint, but DNNs can exhibit high confidence on incorrect predictions. Neural collapse is a geometric phenomenon where features and classifier weights align symmetrically during training, leading to structured decision boundaries.

## [Arabic KG Beats Cross-Lingual English KG for Implicit Aspect ID](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20056) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study shows that a native Arabic knowledge graph outperforms a cross-lingual English KG for implicit aspect identification in Arabic sentiment analysis, with micro-F1 gains of +0.199 and +0.251 on two benchmarks. This finding provides practical guidance for practitioners building ABSA systems for low-resource languages, showing that a smaller native KG can be more effective than a larger cross-lingual KG. It also highlights the importance of task-specific fine-tuning over zero-shot prompting for morphologically rich languages like Arabic. The study compared two strategies within a hybrid pipeline on three Arabic benchmarks (M-ABSA, SemEval-2016 Arabic, HAAD). Task-specific fine-tuning of an 8B-parameter LLM raised explicit-extraction micro-F1 from ≤0.13 (zero-shot) to 0.66-0.76 on M-ABSA and SemEval-2016.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Aspect-based sentiment analysis (ABSA) aims to identify aspects (e.g., 'food', 'service') and their sentiment in text. Implicit aspects are not explicitly mentioned but inferred from opinion cues, often requiring external knowledge like a knowledge graph (KG). For low-resource languages, practitioners face a choice between reusing a mature English KG via cross-lingual embeddings or building a smaller native KG.

## [RL Improves Selective Evidence Adoption in RAG LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20090) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SelectBench, a benchmark for selective evidence adoption, and applied DAPO reinforcement learning to post-train Qwen3.5-4B, achieving modest improvements in strict success rate from 22.46% to 26.46% while reducing forbidden-content adoption. This work addresses a critical safety and reliability issue in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems, where LLMs must selectively adopt useful evidence while rejecting harmful or deceptive content, which is essential for trustworthy deployment in real-world applications. The improvements were modest and did not survive Holm correction, indicating the need for stronger reward shaping or more training iterations; prompt-injection following did not improve, and general capabilities on MMLU and HotpotQA were preserved.

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines LLMs with external knowledge retrieval, but retrieved contexts often mix useful evidence with misleading or harmful content. Selective evidence adoption is the ability to pick relevant information while rejecting deceptive or unsafe content, which is critical for reliable RAG systems. DAPO (Decoupled Clip and Dynamic Sampling Policy Optimization) is an open-source reinforcement learning algorithm designed for large-scale LLM training.

## [ENTRAP-VL: Probing Dual Contextual Entrainment in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ENTRAP-VL, a manually curated dataset of 1,500 items across eight categories designed to probe contextual entrainment in vision-language models (VLMs). It argues that studying this phenomenon requires a dual-modality instrument beyond simply porting text-only benchmarks. Contextual entrainment is a newly identified phenomenon that can affect model robustness and safety, and its study in VLMs has been largely unexamined. ENTRAP-VL provides a structured benchmark for the community to rigorously investigate this phenomenon, potentially leading to more reliable multimodal AI systems. The dataset is organized by a taxonomy spanning two axes: the association of context with the item and its relationship to truth. It is split into a textual-entrainment stream (eight context conditions) and a visual-entrainment stream (three context conditions), enabling independent probing of each modality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Contextual entrainment refers to the tendency of a model to let auxiliary context in its input pull its output, regardless of relevance or truth. It was first identified and mechanistically explained in unimodal language models. The move to VLMs is substantive because entrainment becomes a dual phenomenon, drivable independently by textual and visual context, and introduces a veracity distinction (context false of the depicted scene but possible in the world) that has no counterpart in unimodal settings.

StreamHOI introduces a low-latency streaming framework for long-duration human-object interaction (HOI) video generation, achieving 17.6 FPS with 0.75s first-chunk latency. It uses interaction-aware temporal memory adaptation, including offline block profiling and bias-guided memory-specialized training. This work addresses a critical gap in real-time interactive video generation, enabling applications like virtual reality and gaming where long-duration HOI videos must be generated with low latency. It outperforms existing offline methods in both quality and efficiency. The framework introduces a memory distance scaling module to strengthen long-range access to early interaction states. It also performs offline HOI-aware block profiling to match memory composition with transformer block behavior.

## [ELSAA: Hybrid Sparse-Low-Rank Attention for Efficient Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20214) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ELSAA proposes a dual-branch attention approximation that combines sparse high-similarity interactions with low-rank global interactions, avoiding materialization of the full quadratic attention matrix. This work addresses the quadratic complexity bottleneck of Transformer attention, enabling longer-context training while preserving both sharp token-level interactions and broad contextual mixing, which is crucial for scaling LLMs. ELSAA introduces a denominator-aware fusion term that scales the sparse branch according to its estimated attention mass relative to the low-rank branch, addressing normalization discrepancies between the two branches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer attention computes pairwise similarities between all query-key pairs, resulting in O(N^2) complexity. Existing efficient methods either impose sparsity (each query attends to few keys) or use low-rank/kernel sketches to compress global interactions. ELSAA combines both approaches in a principled way.

Researchers introduced DQAOA-GPT, a hybrid framework that combines distributed quantum approximate optimization with GPT-based quantum circuit generation, eliminating the need for iterative variational optimization. This approach significantly reduces computational cost while maintaining solution quality, addressing a key bottleneck in variational quantum algorithms and paving the way for larger-scale combinatorial optimization in hybrid HPC-QC environments. DQAOA-GPT was benchmarked on dense HUBO optimization problems with up to 100 decision variables, showing larger acceleration for larger sub-problem sizes. The framework uses a trained generative model to directly produce high-quality circuits for decomposed sub-problems.

DQAOA decomposes large problems into smaller sub-problems, and GPT-based circuit generation replaces iterative optimization.

Researchers introduced the Maskability Index (MI), a metric that predicts whether masked or prefix prompting works better for extracting knowledge from pretrained language models like T5 and BERT. MI helps select optimal prompting templates for few-shot knowledge extraction, improving performance in low-resource settings and reducing trial-and-error in prompt engineering. MI is computed from differences in DepthRank scores between masked and unmasked templates, and was validated on the ATOMIC2020 knowledge base completion benchmark, showing positive correlation with downstream generation performance.

## [Courteous Anticipatory Planning for Multi-Robot Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20289) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced courteous anticipatory planning, a method where a robot selects plans that minimize both immediate cost and expected future cost for all robots in a shared environment, using per-robot learned estimators. This approach reduces total cost by up to 17.41% over myopic planning in lengthy task sequences, improving long-term efficiency in multi-robot systems without combinatorial complexity. The method was evaluated in two persistent PDDL domains: a home environment with two robots and a restaurant with three robots, achieving cost reductions of 10.43% and 17.41% respectively against myopic planning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-robot task planning in persistent shared environments requires robots to consider future tasks and other robots' constraints. Standard planners solve each task in isolation, leading to compounding side effects. Courteous anticipatory planning uses factored estimators to avoid joint rollouts and supports modular deployment.

## [Condition Dropout Boosts RGB-D Segmentation Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20326) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Condition Dropout (ConD), a continued-training paradigm that improves RGB-D semantic segmentation robustness when one modality (RGB or depth) is missing, without sacrificing full-modality accuracy. This addresses a practical and underexplored problem in real-world deployment of RGB-D segmentation models, where sensor failures or occlusions often cause missing modalities, leading to severe performance degradation. ConD freezes original encoders and trains copied encoders with zero-initialized feature injection, randomly simulating complete, RGB-missing, and depth-missing inputs during a second training stage. Experiments on NYU-Depth V2 and SUN RGB-D show improved robustness and even slight gains when modalities are complete.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: RGB-D semantic segmentation uses both RGB color images and depth maps to understand scenes. Most models assume both modalities are always available, but in practice, one may be missing due to sensor issues. Condition Dropout is a simple continued-training method that helps models handle such missing modalities effectively.

Researchers introduced DEED, a data-efficient post-training and experience-driven learning framework that improves Vision-Language-Action (VLA) humanoid robot performance in real-world retail tasks, demonstrated on a supermarket chip-restocking task using a Unitree G1-Edu robot and the GR00T N1.6 foundation model. This work addresses the critical lab-to-store gap for humanoid robots, showing that careful data design and targeted post-training can transform a failing policy into a competent real-world system using only a single GPU, which could accelerate deployment of humanoid robots in retail and other service industries. DEED comprises three components: a data-efficient post-training pipeline with control-frequency alignment and task-relevant visual highlighting, an experience-driven refinement adapted from RECAP using a text-based advantage prefix and vision-language value function, and a latent-space analysis tool for studying distribution behavior.

This survey systematically reviews 60 semantic models and over 20 ontology-based use cases for building energy management, and introduces a new metric called Ontology Evidence Completeness (OEC) to assess how well studies map operational concepts to ontology classes. Semantic interoperability is a critical barrier to scalable, autonomous building energy management; this survey provides a structured understanding of current capabilities and gaps, guiding future ontology development and integration efforts. The survey finds that physical building structure, technical systems, and sensing devices are well-covered, while abstract concepts like key performance indicators, control logic, and optimization tasks are less consistently represented. Applied studies often rely on ontology reuse, integration, or extension to fill gaps.

ForenAgent is a multi-round interactive framework that enables multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to autonomously generate, execute, and iteratively refine Python-based low-level tools for interpretable image forgery detection. It uses a two-stage training pipeline combining Cold Start and Reinforcement Fine-Tuning, and introduces FABench, a dataset of 100k images and 200k QA pairs. This work bridges the gap between low-level artifact analysis and high-level semantic reasoning in image forensics, offering a more flexible and interpretable approach. It could significantly improve automated forgery detection in security-critical applications like digital media authentication and legal evidence verification. The framework employs a dynamic reasoning loop with four stages: global perception, local focusing, iterative probing, and holistic adjudication. ForenAgent integrates 12 state-of-the-art image forensics tools with standardized interfaces, and the code will be released after review.

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can process both visual and textual information, and recent advances enable them to use external tools like Python code to perform specialized tasks. ForenAgent leverages this capability to let MLLMs autonomously create and refine tools for forgery analysis.

PedNStream extends LTM with stochastic link dynamics to represent local flow variation, and includes modular controller interfaces for gating, flow separation, and route guidance.

## [AutoVSR: Automated Circuit Expression Generation from Schematics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoVSR is a novel framework that combines Vision Language Models (VLMs) with symbolic solvers to automatically generate accurate symbolic expressions from circuit schematic images, achieving accuracy improvements of 30–59% over end-to-end VLM approaches. This work bridges the gap between visual circuit diagrams and formal symbolic analysis, potentially accelerating electronic design automation and reducing manual effort in circuit verification and optimization. AutoVSR introduces an executable intermediate representation (Executable IR) constructed via component rule retrieval and verification-based feedback, and uses a symbolic solver as a planning agent with a symbolic tool library for reliable multi-step derivation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic expressions are mathematical formulas that describe circuit behavior, essential for analysis and design. Deriving them from schematic images is challenging because it requires both accurate visual recognition of components and correct multi-step algebraic manipulation. Traditional methods rely on manual effort or end-to-end deep learning, which often lack precision. AutoVSR addresses this by combining VLMs for visual understanding with symbolic solvers for rigorous reasoning.

This work addresses the challenge of creating truly human-like private chat systems, which require coherent persona, memory, and timing—aspects often neglected in current chatbots. The framework's diagnostic alignment approach could improve the naturalness and consistency of conversational AI. AnthroDial uses a role-conditioned scheduled dialogue runtime, an executable benchmark with L0 validity gate and ten evaluation dimensions, and a post-training pipeline that filters 16,436 examples for SFT and applies GRPO with a ZPD-aware reward using Kalman-filtered capability estimates. The best model, Qwen3.6-27B-SFT+RL, achieved 39.00% strict accuracy and an overall score of 98.5.

Kalman filtering is a technique for estimating unknown variables from noisy measurements over time. AnthroDial applies these concepts to dialogue generation, evaluation, and alignment.

Researchers propose PGTT, a deep reinforcement learning method for legged robots that uses phase-guided reward shaping instead of oscillator or inverse kinematics priors, achieving higher success rates on challenging terrains. PGTT reduces inductive bias compared to prior methods, enabling better adaptability across robot morphologies and improved robustness to perception noise, which could accelerate deployment of legged robots in real-world environments. PGTT encodes per-leg phase as a cubic Hermite spline, adapts swing height to local heightmap statistics, and adds a swing-phase contact penalty, while the policy acts directly in joint space for morphology-agnostic deployment.

CGCE operates only on unsafe text embeddings at inference time, leaving the original model weights untouched, and has been successfully applied to various modern T2I and T2V models. CGCE introduces a classifier-guided inference-time approach to overcome these limitations.

## [LatentLens Reveals Interpretable Visual Tokens in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00462) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LatentLens is a novel interpretability method that maps visual token representations in vision-language models to natural language descriptions via nearest-neighbor search in a contextualized text corpus. This method demonstrates that visual tokens in LLMs are highly interpretable across all layers, challenging previous assumptions and opening new avenues for understanding how LLMs process visual information. LatentLens was evaluated on 15 different VLMs and outperformed methods like LogitLens, showing that the majority of visual tokens are interpretable at every layer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are created by mapping visual tokens from a vision encoder into the embedding space of a large language model (LLM), often via a simple MLP. Understanding what these visual tokens represent at each layer is crucial for model interpretability, but prior methods like LogitLens often underestimated their interpretability.

## [NeuroSymActive: Differentiable Neural-Symbolic KGQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15353) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeuroSymActive introduces a modular framework that combines a differentiable neural-symbolic reasoning layer with an active, value-guided exploration controller for Knowledge Graph Question Answering (KGQA). This approach addresses the key challenge of integrating neural models with structured knowledge, achieving strong answer accuracy while reducing expensive graph lookups and model calls compared to retrieval-augmented baselines. The framework uses soft-unification symbolic modules, a neural path evaluator, and a Monte-Carlo style exploration policy that prioritizes high-value path expansions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge Graph Question Answering (KGQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge graphs. Traditional methods either naively embed graph facts into prompts, causing inefficiency, or rely on purely symbolic search, lacking gradient-based refinement. NeuroSymActive bridges this gap by making the reasoning process differentiable and incorporating active exploration.

## [AdvSynGNN: Robust GNNs via Adversarial Synthesis and Self-Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17071) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AdvSynGNN is a novel graph neural network architecture that combines adversarial synthesis, transformer-based heterophily handling, and self-corrective label refinement to improve node classification robustness against structural noise and non-homophilous topologies. This work addresses two critical weaknesses of standard GNNs—sensitivity to structural noise and poor performance on heterophilous graphs—which are common in real-world applications like social networks and biological data, potentially enabling more reliable graph-based learning. The framework uses a generative adversarial component to identify potential connectivity alterations while a discriminator enforces global coherence, and a residual correction scheme guided by per-node confidence metrics for label refinement.

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) are powerful tools for learning on graph-structured data but often degrade under structural noise or when connected nodes have different labels (heterophily). Adversarial training and label propagation are existing techniques that AdvSynGNN integrates in a unified architecture to overcome these limitations.

## [SubQuad: Near-Quadratic-Free Structure Inference for Immune Repertoires](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SubQuad introduces an end-to-end pipeline that combines MinHash prefiltering, GPU-accelerated affinity kernels, learned multimodal fusion, and fairness-constrained clustering to achieve near-subquadratic structure inference for adaptive immune repertoires. This work addresses two critical bottlenecks in population-scale immune repertoire analysis: the near-quadratic cost of pairwise affinity evaluations and dataset imbalances that obscure rare but clinically important clonotypes, enabling scalable and bias-aware mining for vaccine and biomarker discovery. SubQuad uses compact MinHash prefiltering to sharply reduce candidate comparisons, a differentiable gating module for adaptive weighting of alignment and embedding channels, and an automated calibration routine to enforce proportional representation of rare antigen-specific subgroups.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Adaptive immune repertoire analysis involves comparing millions of receptor sequences to understand immune responses. Traditional pairwise affinity evaluation scales quadratically with sample size, making population-scale studies computationally prohibitive. MinHash is a locality-sensitive hashing technique that estimates similarity efficiently, and fairness-constrained clustering ensures minority subgroups are not overlooked.

## [DocShield: Unified AI Framework for Text Forgery Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02694) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DocShield introduces a unified framework for text-centric image forgery analysis that integrates detection, localization, and explanation via evidence-grounded agentic reasoning. It leverages a novel Cross-Cues-aware Chain of Thought (CCT) mechanism and a Weighted Multi-Task Reward for GRPO-based optimization. This work addresses a critical gap in document forensics by moving beyond visual-only methods to incorporate logical reasoning, significantly improving detection accuracy. With a 41.4% F1 improvement over specialized frameworks and 23.4% over GPT-4o, it sets a new benchmark for AI document safety. DocShield's CCT mechanism iteratively cross-validates visual anomalies with textual semantics, enabling implicit agentic reasoning. The framework also introduces RealText-V1, a multilingual dataset with pixel-level manipulation masks and expert-level explanations, which will be publicly released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Text-centric image forgeries, such as manipulated documents or screenshots, are increasingly realistic due to generative AI. Existing forensic methods rely mainly on visual cues and treat detection, localization, and explanation as separate tasks, limiting reliability and interpretability. DocShield unifies these tasks using visual-logical co-reasoning.

## [SynSur: End-to-End Pipeline for Synthetic Industrial Defect Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26633) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynSur is an end-to-end generative pipeline that combines Vision-Language-Model-based prompt construction, LoRA-adapted diffusion, mask-guided inpainting, and automatic annotation to produce synthetic industrial surface defects with no manual labeling effort. This pipeline addresses the critical data scarcity problem in industrial defect detection, where defects are rare and annotations require expert knowledge, potentially reducing the cost and effort of building inspection systems. SynSur was evaluated on BSData (ball screw pitting) and MSD scratch datasets using YOLOX, YOLOv26, and LW-DETR detectors; synthetic-only training did not match real data, but augmenting scarce real sets with synthetic samples significantly reduced the performance gap.

## [Tunable MAGMAX: Preference-Aware Model Merging for Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20803) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Tunable MAGMAX introduces a preference vector that controls the number of elements selected from each task vector during model merging, enabling preference-aware control of task-specific performance in continual learning. The method also automatically constructs appropriate preference vectors using small amounts of target environment data. This work addresses a practical gap in deployment flexibility for continual learning models, allowing users to tailor merged models to different deployment environments or user preferences. It extends the state-of-the-art MAGMAX method with a simple yet effective mechanism for trade-off control. The preference vector controls the number of top-magnitude elements selected from each task vector, and the method can automatically construct it using a small validation set from the target environment. Experiments show Tunable MAGMAX achieves superior or comparable performance to baselines on continual learning benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to train models on a sequence of tasks without forgetting previously learned knowledge, a challenge known as catastrophic forgetting. Model merging techniques like MAGMAX combine task-specific parameters (e.g., via maximum magnitude selection) to achieve strong CL performance, but they typically optimize for average performance across tasks without considering deployment preferences.

This paper introduces a lightweight graph structure and agentic toolset for retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that reduces hallucinations in complex question answering. Evaluated on the MoNaCo benchmark, the approach halves the number of hallucinated answers and achieves the highest fine-grained truthfulness score. Hallucinations remain a critical challenge for LLMs in real-world applications, especially when answering complex questions that require reasoning across many documents. This work demonstrates that a simple graph-based RAG approach can significantly improve factual correctness without expensive fine-tuning, making it practical for deployment. The system uses a curated subset of English Wikipedia articles as the structured dataset, combining vector search and graph query tools. The graph schema is lightweight and simple, yet the approach achieves a modest increase in token usage while halving hallucinations.

## [BES: Adaptive Contrastive Learning for Graph Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20283) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Boundary Embedding Shaping (BES), an adaptive contrastive learning plug-in for GNNs that selectively suppresses spurious structural noise at decision boundaries to improve graph structural disentanglement. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in GNNs—boundary node vulnerability—and achieves consistent improvements in node classification (up to 5.0% on WikiCS) and link prediction, advancing graph representation learning. BES is a plug-in module that minimally perturbs model parameters while focusing on boundary-region entanglement. Experiments show BES boosts GCN performance by an average of 3.3% in node classification and achieves superior accuracy in link prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) aggregate neighbor information for node classification, but spurious correlations from semantically irrelevant neighbors can contaminate embeddings, especially near class boundaries. Existing robust GNN methods treat all nodes uniformly, ignoring boundary-specific vulnerabilities. Graph structural disentanglement aims to separate these spurious correlations from meaningful features.

## [DART-VLN: Training-Free Test-Time Control for Discrete VLN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DART-VLN introduces two lightweight, training-free techniques—Test-Time Memory Decay and Anti-Loop Regularization—to improve discrete vision-language navigation agents at test time without retraining. This work addresses common failure modes in memory-based discrete VLN agents, such as stale memory and inefficient backtracking, making navigation more reliable and efficient without additional training cost. Test-Time Memory Decay reweights memory readout to suppress stale evidence, while Anti-Loop Regularization penalizes immediate reversals during action selection. Experiments on R2R and REVERIE show improved trajectory efficiency and navigation performance.

A new framework proposes endpoint-authenticated KV cache and AES-GCM encryption to enable privacy-preserving edge-cloud collaborative inference for large language models, reducing per-token latency by up to 46.1% and downlink payloads by up to 67.4% over baseline split inference. This addresses the critical trilemma of latency, hardware constraints, and privacy in on-device LLM inference, enabling practical deployment on consumer and embedded devices without exposing user data to the cloud. It supports heterogeneous devices including CPU-only, GPU-equipped, and embedded systems via optimized streaming and ONNX deployment.

Speculative decoding uses a small draft model to propose tokens that a larger model verifies in parallel, reducing latency without altering output distribution.

## [Auditing Attribution Faithfulness in Echocardiography Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study audits attribution faithfulness for deep video models estimating left-ventricular ejection fraction from echocardiography, finding that while spatial grounding is accurate, temporal attribution is only slightly above chance. The paper reveals a pronounced asymmetry between spatial and temporal faithfulness that persists regardless of training convergence. This work highlights a critical limitation in current interpretability techniques for medical video models: spatial faithfulness does not imply temporal faithfulness. The findings underscore the need for temporal-specific evaluation in high-stakes clinical AI, where identifying the correct frames (end-systole and end-diastole) is as important as localizing the left ventricle. The study evaluated two architecturally distinct models—a self-supervised VideoMAE transformer with Chefer relevance propagation and a Kinetics-pretrained R(2+1)D convolutional network with Grad-CAM—on the EchoNet-Dynamic dataset. Temporal reliance was measured via tubelet occlusion, showing only 1.05x chance for VideoMAE and 1.15x for R(2+1)D, compared to spatial faithfulness of 3.04x and 3.76x chance respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Left-ventricular ejection fraction (EF) is a key clinical metric for cardiac function, defined by the difference between end-systolic (ES) and end-diastolic (ED) volumes. Deep video models can estimate EF from echocardiography with near-expert accuracy, but ensuring they rely on the correct spatial and temporal regions is critical for clinical trust. Post-hoc attribution methods like Grad-CAM and Layer-wise Relevance Propagation (LRP) are commonly used to explain model decisions, but their faithfulness has not been rigorously evaluated for temporal aspects in medical video.

## [Twin Cycle Autoencoder for Facial AU Detection in DMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16760) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose the Twin Cycle Autoencoder (TCA), a spatiotemporal architecture that disentangles appearance and dynamics from unlabeled driving video for facial action unit detection, achieving consistent improvements over baselines on DISFA, BP4D, and a naturalistic driving dataset. This work advances driver monitoring systems by enabling robust detection of subtle and transient facial AUs (e.g., fatigue, yawning) under challenging real-world conditions, supporting safer ADAS. The self-supervised approach reduces reliance on expensive labeled data. TCA comprises two coupled cycle-consistent autoencoders: a Spatial Cycle Autoencoder for appearance-identity disentanglement and a Temporal Cycle Autoencoder for capturing AU onset-apex-offset dynamics. The model runs in real-time on an embedded Jetson Xavier NX platform.

**Background**: Facial Action Units (AUs) are anatomically defined muscle movements that form the basis of the Facial Action Coding System (FACS). Driver monitoring systems use facial cues to infer states like drowsiness, but existing AU detectors often treat spatial and temporal aspects separately. Self-supervised learning leverages unlabeled data by creating supervisory signals from the data itself.

## [ChemHyperMag: Physics-informed hypergraph for ADMET prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18332) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ChemHyperMag, a physics-informed magnetic hypergraph learning method that improves ADMET prediction by modeling functional group hypergraphs and using a Hermitian magnetic Laplacian to capture asymmetric interactions and nonreversible dynamics. Accurate ADMET prediction is critical for drug discovery, and ChemHyperMag outperforms recent methods with fewer labeled samples and no conformers, offering a scalable and interpretable approach that could accelerate early-stage drug development. ChemHyperMag builds a functional group hypergraph from rings, BRICS fragments, Bemis-Murcko scaffolds, and bonds, and defines a potential-driven nonreversible flow guided by electronegativity and Gasteiger partial charges, encoded by a Hermitian magnetic Laplacian and processed with a magnetic Chebyshev encoder.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: ADMET prediction assesses how a drug is absorbed, distributed, metabolized, excreted, and its toxicity. Traditional molecular graphs use undirected pairwise edges, missing asymmetric interactions and motif-level effects from functional groups. The magnetic Laplacian, originating from quantum physics, encodes directional information via complex phases, enabling the model to capture nonreversible dynamics.

## [FSDBN: Foreground-Aware EEG-Visual Alignment via Dynamic Brain Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18344) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose FSDBN, a unified framework for EEG-based visual decoding that introduces Semantic-Consistent Saliency Alignment and Semantic-Prior Dynamic Gating Foreground Fusion to separate foreground from background, and models EEG signals as adaptive spatiotemporal brain networks. On zero-shot brain-to-image retrieval, FSDBN achieves 69.0% top-1 and 92.2% top-5 accuracy, outperforming previous state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses two critical challenges in EEG-based visual decoding: foreground-background perceptual asymmetry and dynamic brain connectivity. By improving the alignment between neural signals and visual semantics, FSDBN could advance brain-computer interfaces and enable more accurate decoding of visual experiences from EEG. The framework uses Semantic-Consistent Saliency Alignment to separate foreground regions under joint saliency and semantic constraints, and Semantic-Prior Dynamic Gating Foreground Fusion to adaptively regulate foreground and background feature contributions. EEG signals are modeled as adaptive spatiotemporal brain networks whose functional connectivity dynamically reorganizes to capture neural responses to salient foregrounds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: EEG-based visual decoding aims to reconstruct or retrieve visual stimuli from brain activity recorded via electroencephalography (EEG). Traditional methods often treat all visual regions equally, ignoring that humans focus more on foreground objects, and they fail to capture the rapidly changing brain connectivity patterns during visual attention. FSDBN leverages saliency maps and dynamic brain network modeling to overcome these limitations.

FraudShield AI, a hybrid LSTM-GNN framework, is proposed for financial fraud detection, integrating temporal sequences with graph topological features and using Focal Loss to handle extreme class imbalance. This approach shifts fraud detection from isolated transaction analysis to network-level forensics, improving detection of sophisticated patterns like smurfing and layering, which are hard for traditional models. ## [AI Audit Finds Candle-Based Crypto Trading Models Unprofitable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19453) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A rigorous AI-assisted audit of candle-based machine learning models for cryptocurrency trading on Binance Spot found that all tested strategies failed to generate positive returns after accounting for transaction costs. This negative result challenges the common belief that machine learning can easily extract profits from candlestick patterns in crypto markets, highlighting the importance of realistic cost assumptions and rigorous backtesting. The strongest later-period evidence showed a mandatory daily selector lost 6.72% over 19 cycles at a 31-bps cost, with only 3 wins and 16 losses; a Gurgul-inspired model achieved high ROC AUC (0.874/0.896) but low average precision (0.134/0.116) and lost 44.30%.

<a id="item-244"></a>
## [Benchmark of Intensity Normalization for Knee MRI Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20028) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study systematically compares seven intensity normalization methods for 3D knee MRI segmentation using a 3D U-Net, finding that Z-score, Nyul histogram matching, and CLAHE improve cross-domain generalizability on external data. Robust out-of-the-box performance is critical for clinical deployment of deep learning models in medical imaging, and this work provides practical guidance on choosing normalization methods to mitigate domain shift. Models were trained on the IWOAI 2019 dataset and evaluated on internal and external (SKM-TEA) test sets; performance differences were small internally but significant externally, though still limited compared to the overall domain shift effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Intensity normalization is a preprocessing step that reduces variability in MRI intensities across scanners and protocols. Common methods include Z-score scaling, histogram matching (e.g., Nyul), and adaptive histogram equalization (CLAHE). Domain shift refers to the performance drop when a model is applied to data from a different distribution than its training set.

## [Study: GenAI Drives Traffic to Academic Library Resources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20328) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study analyzing web analytics from August 2023 to October 2025 found that generative AI platforms like ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Gemini are increasingly driving users to academic library resources, especially after the integration of linked citation features. This research highlights a new discovery pathway for academic content, emphasizing the need for libraries to optimize metadata and open access to remain visible in AI-driven search ecosystems. The study found that a substantial portion of AI-mediated traffic reached institutional repositories, primarily accessing electronic theses and dissertations, and that resources with structured metadata and stable permalinks were more effectively surfaced.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI platforms like ChatGPT and Perplexity can now cite sources, including academic papers. Structured metadata (e.g., standardized fields for title, author, DOI) helps AI systems accurately retrieve and link to resources. Open access materials are more likely to be surfaced because they are freely available without paywalls.

## [HIPAA-Compliant AI-Augmented LIMS for Clinical Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20382) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FMRP-LEAN, a HIPAA-compliant, AI-augmented Laboratory Information Management System (LIMS) architecture that uses a finite-state workflow model to optimize clinical assay workflows, specifically for Luminex-based FMRP quantification. This architecture addresses critical gaps in clinical biomarker workflows, such as manual tracking and delayed reporting, by providing deterministic state visibility and AI-augmented QC, which can reduce operational risk and improve cross-team coordination in regulated healthcare environments. The system uses a self-hosted Supabase/PostgreSQL stack with encrypted tunneling, bi-directional REDCap synchronization, and a unified MRN-UUIDv7 identifier framework with QR-based tracking. The AI module operates only on aggregate projections with deterministic fallback guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical biomarker workflows in translational research often rely on spreadsheets and manual QC, leading to inefficiencies and errors. LIMS (Laboratory Information Management Systems) are designed to manage lab data and workflows, but many lack HIPAA compliance and AI integration. FMRP is a protein linked to Fragile X syndrome, and its quantification via Luminex assays is a multi-day process requiring strict data governance.

## [Agent-Based Model Simulates Low-Emission Fertilizer Adoption on Dairy Farms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03648) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed an agent-based model (ABM) that simulates the adoption of low-emission fertilizers among 295 Irish dairy farms over 15 years, achieving high predictive accuracy (R² = 0.979) and validated against observed data using a Kolmogorov-Smirnov test. This work provides a validated tool for policymakers to evaluate the diffusion of climate strategies before implementation, addressing the socio-technical dynamics of agricultural decarbonization rather than purely economic incentives. The model incorporates farm heterogeneity, social network communication, and policy interventions like subsidies and carbon taxes, with adoption dynamics fitting Rogers logistic curves and reaching a 91% saturation plateau.

Low-emission fertilizers reduce greenhouse gas emissions but adoption depends on economic, social, and policy factors.

<a id="item-248"></a>
## [Pre-Deployment Complexity Estimation for Federated Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28282) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a classifier-agnostic framework that combines intrinsic data properties (dimensionality, sparsity, heterogeneity) with client-distribution composition to estimate learning complexity in federated perception systems before training. Experiments on three MNIST variants show strong negative correlations between the complexity metric and federated accuracy. This framework provides a practical diagnostic tool for resource planning, dataset assessment, and feasibility evaluation in edge-deployed perception systems, addressing a gap in pre-deployment complexity estimation for federated learning. It could help practitioners avoid costly trial-and-error in distributed training. The framework is classifier-agnostic and validated only on MNIST variants, so its generalizability to real-world perception tasks remains unproven. The complexity metric combines intrinsic data properties and client-distribution composition, with separate components correlating with communication effort.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains models across distributed edge devices without sharing raw data, preserving privacy. Perception systems (e.g., object detection) are critical for edge AI but face challenges like heterogeneous data and limited resources. Pre-deployment complexity estimation helps predict training difficulty and resource needs, but existing methods are often task-specific or require training.

## [Survival Benchmark for Temporal Dropout Risk in Learning Analytics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08870) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study proposes a survival-oriented benchmark for temporal dropout risk prediction using the OULAD dataset, comparing dynamic weekly models (Family A) and static early-window models (Family B) across predictive performance, ablation, explainability, and calibration. This benchmark provides a unified, multi-dimensional evaluation protocol that emphasizes temporal interpretability and calibration, moving beyond simple discrimination metrics and highlighting the dominant temporal-behavioral nature of dropout risk. Within Family B, Random Survival Forest achieved the highest time-dependent concordance and lowest Brier scores; within Family A, Poisson Piecewise-Exponential had the lowest integrated Brier score. No-refit bootstrap resampling indicates these are directional signals, not strict superiority claims.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Student dropout prediction in learning analytics often uses static features and discrimination-focused metrics. Survival analysis models time-to-event data and can handle temporal dynamics. The OULAD dataset includes student demographics, course interactions, and VLE clickstreams, making it suitable for temporal dropout modeling.

## [Auditable Policy-Simulation Framework for Student Dropout](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08874) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a temporal modeling framework using LMS engagement data and discrete-time survival models to simulate counterfactual policy interventions on student dropout risk in higher education. 该框架使机构能够在实施前审计和比较不同辍学预防政策的潜在影响，为资源分配和学生支持提供数据驱动的方法。 The model achieves row-level AUCs of 0.8350 (train) and 0.8405 (test) using penalized, class-balanced logistic regression on person-period rows, with scenario-indexed policy layers producing survival contrasts under explicit trigger/schedule contracts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 23, 04:00

**Background**: Discrete-time survival models predict the hazard of an event in pre-specified time intervals by applying binary classification to person-period data. Counterfactual policy simulation evaluates what would happen under alternative intervention scenarios, but causal identification requires additional assumptions. This work combines both to create an auditable framework for dropout risk.
